E. PREFACE. |
Whenever the phrase, “ Approved June 1st, 1869,”
occurs, it means that the sections preceding it, up to
| the commencement of the chapter, or to the next pre-
ceding date of approval, are taken from the pamphiet,
“Revised Ordinances of the City of Los Angeles.
passed and approved June Ist, 1869,’ and not the
date of their original approval by the Common Cozun-
eiland Mayor, (as many of the original copies are lost.)
) All other sections or ordinances marked approved on a —
) different date, are the actual dates of approval by the
municipal authorities. The foot notes to the Charter
) eal] attention to amendments and construction by the
Supreme Court to the sections preceding. Appendix
No. 1, contains Ordinances mainly pertaining to Land,
) Streets, Title to Land, and to Maps of Zanjas and
Streets, some of which have been repealed, but never-
_ theless deemed important.
, Appexdix No. 2 coutainsthe reference to the Statutes,
and ynotes the sections authorizing and directing the
Council to make levies for the various Funds. It also,
in like manner, makes reference to the Statutes, and
quotes the sections thereof, directing how the various
Debts created under them may be gradually liquidated.
This Appendix will be found to be essentially conve-
nient for the municipal authoysiies; to all persons desir-
ing to readily acquaint ‘neal with the condition
of the Public Debt of the city, and the process of its
liquidation.
W. McPuekson,
Compiler,
Attorney at law---Otiice, No. 55, Temple Block.

Sec. 8. The following rults shall be observed in con-
ducting the business of the City Council, viz.:
RULE I.
The President shall take the chair at the hour ap-
pointed for the Council to meet, and shall immediate-
ly call the members to order.
RULE Il.
At the stated meetings of the Council, the reading
of the journal of the preceding, stated and intervening
special m-etings shall firs be in order, unless un: m-
mously dispensed with, and the unfinished business ap-
pearing thereon shall be then disposed of, unless otler-
wise directed by the Council.
RULE III.
After a motion isstated by the Chairman or read by
the Clerk, it may be withdrawn by the mover at any
time before decision or amendment.
RULE Iv.
When demanded by any member present, the ayes
and nays shall be entered on the journal, and they shall
be taken upon the passage of every ordinance and en-
tered on the journal.
RULE V.
No member shall vote when personally interested in
the decision of the question before the Council, nor
shall any member be security ou any bond, note er ob-
ligation given to the city.

See. T1. There shall be an Overseer of Water, to be
appointed by the Council, and approved by the Mayor,
who shall hold office during the pleasure of a majority
of said Council and the Mayor.

Sec. 72. The irrigation of lands shall commence
from the first day of each year, «nd shall continue to
the 31st day of December thereafter.

Sec. 73. It shall be the duty of the Overseer of Wa-
ter, jirsily, to assist and direct the repairs and work up-
on the main canal and branches; to have the supervis-
ion and government of the same, and to take care to
prevent all obstructions interfering with the free pass-
age of the water therein.

Second. To keep a book (which shall be kept open
in the Overseer’s office) in which the names of all per-
sous having purchased water shall be kept, setting
forth the number of days, nights or hours, and on what
wa'er ditch.

Third. To grant punctually the water to the culti-
vators on those days, nights and hours, wherein it has
been set apart to them, and to take care that no one
disturbs them in the free use and enjoyment of the wa-
ter due them.

Fourth. To examine the petitions for water of those :
that may have had the same to use previously, and to
see whether such petitions are made through necessity ;
and if otherwise, he may postpone granting them the
water unti! some subsequent period.

Fifth. To get from the Mayor on the twenty-fifth
day of every month a certain number of water permits,
to be printed in full and for one day each; also, a cer-
tain number for nights and hours; and, also, for: half

Sec. 74. That in future, whenever it shall become
necessary that the main and principal water ditches
shall require cleaning out for the purpose of facilitating
irrigation, it shall be the duty of the Corporation to
have all such water ditches opened and cleaned out at
the cost and charge of the new water fund.

Sec. 76. All complaints that the cultivators of lands
have to make because of injustice done them in the ¢is-
tribution of water, shall be laid before the Mayor, who,
upon hearing the cvuse, shall forthwith decide the case
with as litte expense and delay as possible, and any
person not satistied with lis decision, shall, if th y
choose, appeal to the Common Council, when in ses-
s310n.

Sec. 76. The prices for water for one day, $1 50; for
one night, $1; for each half day, $1; for oue hour, 25

cents. A day’s water shall be from sunrise to sunset
and a half day’s water shall be from sunrise to 12 M.
and from 12 M. until sunset; and the hour shall be
from sunset until one hourthereafter. And no person
shall be entit'ed to water permits until they have paid
for them in advance.

Sec. T7. All persons are prohibited from making or
(damaging zanjas) causing to be made any aperture in
any place where water is drawn out of the river intoa
water canal, or making or causing to be made in any
water cial, raising or causing to be raised any flood
gat23, destroying or causing to be destroyed the same,
placing or causing to be placed any obstruction to the
course of the water in any canal, thereby impeding the
free current and distribution of the same, placing or
causing to be placed anything that shall injure the
banks of the canals or render the water unclean, taking
or using water, destined for irrigation, out of the 7
proper time, to the injury of another, or using water
without a permit and to the injury of another, shall be
fined by the Mayor ina sum not less than ten dollars,
nor more than fifty dollars, at the discretion of the
Mayor.

Sec. 78. Every person who irrigates by taking out
water from the principal of the zanjas, or water
ditches, shall construct a flood-gate in order to pre-
vent the banks or borders from being washed away or
damaged; and if any cultivator shall fail to make such
flood-gate, according to the directions, and to the satis-
faction of the Wa'er Overseer, he shall be deprived of
the use of said water until he erects the sume to the
satisfaction of the Water Overseer.

See. 79. Every person who irrigates by taking out
water from the principal or arms thereof, shall con-
struct a flood-gate in order to prevent the banks or
borders from being washed away or damaged; and if
any cultivator shall fail to make his flood-gate, he shall
be deprived of the use of said water until he erects the
sam: to the satisfaction of the Water Overseer.

Sec. 80. Every person who shall take out water from
any cinal or branch of the same, for irrigating lands,
sha‘! do so at the customary places heretofore used, and
shall not open any new place for that purpose without
the previous consent of the Water Overseer. -

Sec. 81. No person shall make any breach in the
bank of any water canal, or branch thereof, so as to

=== PDF PAGE 59 ===
4
[ 58 ]

cause the water to waste, to the great injury of others;
nor is any person allowed to keep their own or private
water ditches in such bad condition as to cause a waste
of water, either by running on the public roads or
otherwise; and any violation of this section shall sub-
ject the offender to a fine of five dollars for the first of-
fence, and for all subsequent offences not less than ten
nor more than thirty dollars, at the discretion of the
Mayor; and it shall be the duty of the Water Overseer,
or any other public officer, to enforce this ordinance.

Sec. 82. Each cultivator shall prepare his land in
such manner and to the best advantage, and shall make
all such water canals through his land leading from
some main water canal in such mannez as shall be sat-
isfactory to the Overseer of Water; and if the said cul-
tivator shall fail to prepare his land and make such wa-
ter canal through it, as aforesaid, to the full and entire
satisfaction of the said Overseer, then such cultivator
shall not have any water from the said water canals for
irrigation of his land until he so prepare it and make
his said water canals as aforesaid.

Sec. 83. That excepting the four days set apart for
the necessary applications for water and selling water

_- permits, the Water Overseer shall be at his office each
day from 10 to 11 o’clock a. M., and from 1 to 3 o’clock
p.M. of each day, instead of the time specified in sec-
tion twelve of said ordinance to which this is an amend-
ment. That the 24th and 25th of each month shall be
the days for receiving petitions for water on all the
ditches for irrigating purposes; and the 27th and 28th ~-

. shall be the days for selling said water—and all persons
failing to go to the office of the Water Overseer on the
day of sale to get their permits, shall forfeit the same,
and the water will be sold to any person applying for
the same, within the city limits.

Should either of the above mentioned days come on
Sunday, then the next day after said Sunday is to be
the day, either for receiving petitions or selling water.

All ordinances or parts of ordinances in conflict with
this ordinance are hereby repealed, and this ordinance ~
shall be in force from and arter the 24th day of April,
1872, and after its passage, approval and publicaton. Ap-

Sec. 84. The Mayor of the city is directed specially
to have this article efficiently executed in all its parts.

Sec. 85. There shall be, and is hereby established, a
fund to be known and designated as the “Water
Fund.”

‘Sec. 86. The tax called the “water tax,” and all wa-
ter rates, whether for domestic, farming or milling pur-
poses, are hereby withdrawn from the “cash fund” and
made part of the “water fund.”

Sec. 87. All expenses for making bridges, acequias,
flumes, and all expenses pertaining to the water of the
city, shall be drawn from the “new water fund.”

Sec. 88. That from and after the first day of each
month it shall be the duty of the Water Overseer to
make sale of permits for surplus water to be used in
night irrigation, outside the city limits, to persons who
may wish to purchase the same atthe rate of fifty per
cent. more than the rates now fixed by ordinance, out-
side the city limits, and so much of Section Seventeen
of the ordinance to which this is an amendment, as is
in conflict with this section, 1s hereby repealed.

Sec. 89. No person or persons to whom water or per-
mits shall be sold to be used for irrigation within the
city limits, shall in any manner seil or transfer any
right to use such water for the benefit or advantage of
any peason or persons outside of the city limits; and
for any violation of this section, such person shall, upon
conviction, be fined in a sum not exceeding twenty-tive
dollars, with costs of the action, and it shall be the duty
of the Water Overseer to cut off such supply immedi-
ately if the same shall come to his knowledge while
the same is being so used by any one outside the city

limits.

All ordinances or parts of ordinances in conflict with
this ordinance are hereby repealed.

month. Approved March 2, 1872.

thence, along the east sidewalk on Main street to the |
south sidewalk on Sixth street; thence, along the south
side of Sixth street to the west side of Fort street;
thence, along the west side of Fort street to Seventh
street; thence, along the north side of Seventh street
to Olive street, be, and the same is hereby declared a

: sewer and zanja of said city for the express purpose of
carrying off all sewage matter, except, and expressly
providing that it shall not be lawful to use the same for
carrying off any sewage matter, wash, waste or offal
from the gas works in said city. Which said sewer and
zanja shall be known as Number Nine in said city, and
the same be declared a public sewer and zanja. Ap-
proved June 25, 1872.

Sec. 938. A public zanja, acequia, or water course of the
City of Los Angeles is hereby declared and created, the
same running as follows: Commencing on and from the
present Zanza No. 1, at the intersection of the western
line of the land of Charles Baurs with the southern line
of the land of Paul Kern; thence, running through the
land of said Baurs along the northern boundary of said
Baurs, and the southern line of said Kern, course north
80° east, magnetic, six hundred and fifty feet; thence,
along the division line between the land of said Baurs
and the land of John Behn, over the land of sail Baurs,
course, south 15° east, magnetic, three hundred and
twenty-nine feet; thence, along the aforesaid division
line of said Baurs and Behn—course, north 80° east,
magnetic, over the land of said Baurs to his eastern
line, and the western line of the lands of Francisco
Ruiz, four hundred and fifteen feet. The zanja hereby
created, having a width of eight feet, including the
space necessary for the flow of its water, and for drain-
ing and excavating the same; and the line above
specified being the northern and eastern line of such
strip of eight feet.

Sec. 94. A public canal or water-course of the City
of Los Angeles is hereby created and declared, as fol-
lows: Commencing in said city at a point on the hill-
side opposite to and at right-anglea with a point on the
main zanja twenty-seven feet below the dam, the dis-
tance from said point of commencement to the said
point on said zanja being one hundred and twenty-two
feet; and from said point of commencement, following

=== PDF PAGE 63 ===
'
[ 62 ] |
the course laid down in the plot of said canal or water-
course made by William H. Leighton, surveyor, itt
July, 1861, certified to by him on August 3, 1861, and
filed in the office of the Mayor of said city, to a point
in the fractional block next north of block 42, Ord's
city survey; said water-course to be of sufficient width
and depth for the flow of water, not exceeding ten feet
in breadth.

Sec. 95. Any person using the water of said canal or
water-course for any other purpose than domestic uses
shall be punished by a fine in a sum not less than five
nor more than fifty dollars, or by imprisonment not ex-
ceeding ten days, or by both such fine and imprison-
ment.

Sec. 96. Any person throwing any filth or other mat-
ter into said water-course or reservoir attached to the
same, or allowing any filthy or unwholesome matter to
flow into the same, or into any reservoir attached to
the same, or in any way damaging any wheel, ma-
chinery, flume or other appurtenance to the same, shall
be liable to the penalty provided in the preceding sec-
tion.

See. 97. Zanja No. 6, which debouches from zanja
No. 4, on the lands of Rump, and which is by its course
and windings indicated on a map, and by a survey made
by George Hansen, County Surveyor of the county of
Los Angeles, on the seventh day of February, 1864, is
hereby declared to be a public zanja and sewer, with
the rights and privileges of a public zanja. Approved
June 1, 1869.

The Mayor and Gommon Council of the City of Los
Angeles do ordain as follows:

Section 1. That all the right which the City of Los
Angeles now has or may hereafter acquire to the water
flowing. in the ditch, canal reservoir and tunnel, or
which may hereafter flow in the same, of the said Los
Angeles Canal and Reservoir Company, to the extent
of one head of water of two hundred square inches,
cross section, of a constant flow; the use thereof is here-
by granted unto O. Barnard & Brother, and their asso-
clates and assigns, for the purpose of propelling the ma-
chinery of a woolen factory, for the manufacture of
woolen fabrics, and other works, in the City of Los
Angeles, between the prolongation of the lines of
Fourth and Fifth streets, subject to and hereby reserv-

=== PDF PAGE 64 ===
>
[ 63 ]
ing the right of said city to use said water for irrigating
purposes.

Sec. 2. The Mayor of said city is hereby empowered
to execute any instrument or contract on behalf of said
city, upon the foregoing limitations and ¢onditions, to
the said O. Barnard & Brother and their associates and
assigns, by which the use of said water for said purposes
shall be secured to them, and upon the condition that
the said O. Barnard and Brother shall erect a woolen
manufactory at or near the point indicated in Section 1
of this Ordinance, between the prolongation of Fourth
and Fifth streets, in said city, of not less than twelve
thousand dollars in value, and complete the same by
the first day of January, 1873; reserving all the water
which may flow in any canal, ditch or tunnel, which
shall not be needful to said O. Barnard & Brother for ©
their use in propelling their factory, and all the water
which may flow from the said factory, the same at all
times to be free from adulteration by chemical mix-
tures, injurious for irrigation; the said O. Bernard &
Brother and their assigns to keep the said ditch, canal,
reservoir and tunnel in repair, when not used by said
city for irrigation, and said city to keep the same in
repair during the irrigating season; but neither party
to be liable for unavoidable accidents or occurrances.

This ordinance shall be in force from and after its
passage, approval and publication. Approved August
26, 1872.

CRISTOBAL AGUILAR, Mayor.

Sec. 98. The running at large of any horses, jacks,
Jennies, horned cattle, sheep, goats or hogs within the
limits of the City of Los Angeles, upon any of the
streets, alleys, uninclosed lots, or on the river bed with-
in said limits, is a nuisance.

Sec. 99. There shall be a Public Pound Keeper, to
be appointed by the Mayor, with the approval of the
Gommon Council. Before entering upon the discharge

: of his duties, said Pound Keeper shall give bond, with
two sufficient sureties, in the sum of two thousand
($2,000) dollars, to be approved by the Mayor, and
shall be liable thereon for any violation of his duties,
and he shall be subject to removal at the pleasure of
the Council. |

Sec. 100. The Pound Keeper shall provide a corral
at his own expense, 1n which to impound any animals

7 mentioned in Section——-—of this ordinance.

Sec. 101. Any resident of this city, in the actual oc-
cupation of lands within its limits upon which any of
the animals mentioned above may be found actually
trespassing, may take up, and without delay impound
the same in the public pound.

Sec. 102. The Pound Keeper shall keep a record of |
the number and description of all animals taken into
his custody, by whom impounded, with the date of
their receipt, and the date and manner of their disposal;
and shall keep conspicuously posted at the entrance of
the pound a list of all animals therein detained. He
shall also provide necessary subsistance for all animals
while in his custody. .

owner or agent claiming any of the following named
animals impounded, the following charges per diem for

Sec.—. It shall be the duty of the Marshal and
Policemen to take up and impound any of said animals
found at large upon the streets within said limits, and
for each animal so impounded the officer shall receive
the sum of one dollar, to be collected by the Pound
Keeper from the owner of said animal; provided, that
they shall not receive more than ten dollars for im-
pounding any number of animals of any one owner up-
on any single occasion.

Sec. 104. If there be no demand ‘or any of the
animals thus impounded, or if the claimant refuse to
pay the rate of fees and charges allowed for impound-
ing said animals, the Pound Keeper is authorized and
directed to advertise the same for sale by posting up
three written or printed notices in English and Spanish,
one of which shall be placed on the entrance of the
city pound, one on the bulletin board of the Mayor’s
office, the other on the Court House, giving the de-
scription, with the marks and brands of said animal, for
five days; and for each animal so advertised the Pound
Keeper shall be entitled to twenty-five cents, to be col-
lected as hereinafter provided; and on the day adver-
tised shall offer for sale, to the highest bidder, for each
said animal or animals, provided the bid is sufficient, or
more than enough to cover the expenses incurred in im-
pounding and advertising said animals, and after de-
ducting his fees for the whole number of days said ani-
mals were impounded, and for costs of advertising, and
the fee of the officer who may have impounded them,

, shall pay the remainder, if any, at once to the Gity
Treasurer forthe use of the owner, if claimed within
six months thereafter; if not, the same shall be ap-
plied to the use of the City Hospital.

Sec. 105. This Ordinance shall be published for four

weeks, in English and Spanish, in the Los Angeles
Star, and copies thereof posted in three public places
in the city limits, one of which shall be on, the door of
the Mayor’s office.

Article XII,-of the Revised Ordinances of the City
of Los Angeles, passed and approved June 1, 1869, and
all ordinances and parts of ordinances inconsistent with }
this, are hereby repealed.

a suitable brand iron for the city, which he shall de-

Sec. 130. Any person or persons occupying or pos-
sessing lands belonging to the City of Los Angeles,
and desirous of obtaining a title to the same from the
city, shall file a petition therefor, together with a map
and description of the land prayed for, with the Clerk
of the Common Council, at least six days before the
regular session day of the said Gommon Council.

Sec. 131. Immediately on the filing of such petitions
with the Clerk of the Common Council, the Clerk of
said Common Gouncil shall cause to be published in
some newspaper published in the City of Los Angeles,
a notice, for at least five days, consecutively, before the
regular meeting of said Common Council, to the effect
that the petition, or petitions have been tiled with him,
praying for titles to the lands described therein, and
that the same will be had at the regular session of the
Gommon Council. Said notice, in all eases, to contain
a description of the land prayed for, and the name of
the petitioner, together with a notice to all parties in-
terested therein to file their objections, if any they
have, in writing, with the Glerk of the Gommon
Gouncil, at least one day before the regular session of
that body, next after the first publication of such no-
tice or notices. ' 3

deeds to any lands, under the provisions of this ordi-

Sec. 134. The fiscal year of the City of Los Angeles
shall commence on the first day of May and end on the
last day of April of each year.

See. 135. All property within the corporate limits of *
the City of Los Angeles must be assessed at its full
cash value.

Sec. 136. The City Assessor must, between the first
day of May and the first day of August, in each year,
ascertain the names of all taxable inhabitants, and all
the property in the corporate limits subject to taxation,
and must assess such property to the persons who own,
claim, have the possession and control thereof.

Sec. 137. He must exact from each person a state-

ment in writing, showing separately, first, all property
belonging to, claimed by, or in the possession of or un-
der the control or management of such person. Second,
all property belonging to or claimed by, or in the pos-
sessiou or under the control or management of any
firm of which he isa member. Third, all property be-
lounging to, claimed by, or in the possession, or under
the control or management of any corporation of which
such person is President, Secretary, Cashier, or man-
aging agent. Fifth, an exact description of all lands,
improvements and personal property, and deposits of
money, gold dust or bullion, and the names of the per-
sons with whom such deposits are made, and the place
in which they may be found. Sixth, the Mayor and
Gommon Council must furnish the Assessor with
blank forms for the statements provided for in the pre-
ceding section, affixing thereto an affidavit which must
be substantially, as follows:

I,———,, do swear that the above list contains a full
and correct statement of all property subject to taxa-
tion which [, or any firm of which lam a member, or
any corporation, association or company of which I am
President, Secretary, Cashier or managing agent, own,
claim, possess or control, and which is not already as-
sessed.

Sec. 138. The Assessor may fill out the statement at
the time he presents it, or he may deliver it to the per-
son, and require him, within an appointed time, to return
the same, properly filled out.

=== PDF PAGE 79 ===
(TS <] |
ox Sec. 189. The City Assessor shall have the same
power as is provided in Section 3,632, of the Political
Code, in reference to making assessments; provided,
however, before exercising the power as directed in
: said section, the Assessor shall first apply to the Com-
mon Gouncil for authority to proceed in the manner
provided in said section, in all instances when necessity
* requires it, a resort to this section, in order to secure a
proper valuation of property for the purposes of taxa-
tion.

Sec. 140. If any person, after demand made by the
Assessor, neglects or refuses to give, under oath, the
statement herein provided for, the Assessor must note
the refusal on the assessment book, opposite his name,
and must make an estimate of the value of the property
of such person, and the value so fixed hy the Assessor
must not be‘reduced by the Common Council sitting
as a Board of Equalization.

Sec. 141. Whenever the Assessor deems it necessary 7
to obtain an accurate deseription of any tract or lot of
land in the corporate limits, he may require the owner,
or his agent, to furnish the same, with any title papers
he may have in his possession, and if on demand the
owner or agent neglects or refuses to furnish the same,
the Assessor may employ the City Surveyor or Engi-
neer to make out a deseription of the boundaries and
location thereof, and a statement of the quantity of the
land therein, and the expenses of such survey must be
returned by the Assessorto the Mayor and Common
Council, who must add the amount to the taxes as-

| sessed upon the property, which amount so added
must, when collected by the City Tax Collector, be
Si over to said City Surveyor or Engineer, taking
uplicate receipts therefor, one of which he shall re-
turn to the Common Council.

Sec. 142. If the owner or claimant of any property, not
listed, is absent or unknown, the Assessor must make
un estimate of the value of such property, with a de-
scription thereof. If the name of the absent owner is
not known to the Assessor, the property must be as-
sessed to unknown owners.

Sec. 143. When any person is assessed as Trustee,
Executor, Administrator or Guardian, his representa-
tive designation must be added to his name, and the as-
sessment entered on a separate line from his individual

| assessment. .

=== PDF PAGE 80 ===
[ 79 ]

Sec. 144. The owner or holder of stock in any firm
or corporation within the corporate limits, the entire
capital or property whereof is assessed must not be as-
sessed individually for his stock in such corporation.

The property of every firm and corporation must be
assessed in the name of the firm or corporation.

Sec, 145. Any property willfully concealed, removed
or misrepresented by the owner or agent thereof, to
evade taxation, upon discovery must be assessed at not
exceeding thfee times its value, and the assessment so
made must not be reduced by the Common Council,
sitting as a Board of Equalization.

See. 146. The Common Council shall furnish the As-
sessor an assessment book, with appropriate headings,
alphabetically arranged, in which must be listed all
property within the city, and in which must be speci-
fied 1n separate columns under the appropriate head.

1st. The name of the person to whom the property is
assessed.

2d. Lands by metes and bounds, or other description
sufficient to identify, giving an estimate of the number
of acres, or the number of the feet front of each owner
in any block or lot, in accordance with the system of
numbering, as the same may appear upon official maps
of the city, and the improvements thereon, with the
cash value of the real estate, and the cash value of the
improvements thereon.

3d. All personal property showing the number, kind,
amount, quality, but a failure to enumerate in detail
such personal property shall not invalidate the assess-

ment.

4th. The cash value of improvements on real estate
assessed to persons other than the owners of the real
estate.

5th. The cash value of all personal property, exclu-
sive of money.

6th. Amount of money.

7th. The total value of all property.

8th. The total value of all property, after equaliza-
tion by the Commom Gouncil.

I,——~, Assessor of (or Deputy Assessor, as the :

will, nor allowed any one to escape a just and equal as-
sessment through favor or reward. )

must be delivered to the Clerk on or before the 3d day

assessment book, and equalize the assessment! of proj-

sec. 150. The Mayor and Common VUouncil, as a

may subpeena such witnesses, hear and take such evi-

Sec. 153. During the session of the Board, the As-
sessor and his deputy, whose evidence is needed,
must be present, and may make any statement, or
introduce and examine witnesses on questions before
the Board.

Sec. 154. The Board of Equalization may require
the Assessor to enter upon the assessment book any
property which has not been assessed, and any assess-
ment made as prescribed in this section has the same
force and effect as if made by the Assessor, before the
delivery of the assessment book to the Clerk of the
Board; but no person must be assessed under this sec-
tion excejt a resident of the city. In all cases where
the Board either adds to or decreases, or alters. the val-
uation of property made by the Assessor, the Clerk
shall note the changes therein on the assessment book.

Sec. 155. During the session of the Board, it may
direct the Assessor to assess any taxable property that
has escaped assessment, or add to the amount, number .
or quantity of property when a false or incomplete list
has been rendered, and to make and enter new assess-
ments (at the same time cancelling previous entries)
when any assessment made by him is deemed by the
Board incomplete; but the Clerk must notify all per-
sons interested, by letter deposited in the Postofiice,
postpaid in all cases, when the assessment has been in-
creased.

Sec. 158. Every tax so levied shall have the ettect of
a judgment against the person and property taxed, and
every such lien created by this ordinance shall have
the effect and force of an execution duly levied against
all property of the delinquent; the judgment is not sat-
isfied nor the lien removed until the taxes are paid or
the property sold for the payment thereof.

Sec. 159. Every tax due upon personal property is a
lien upon the real property of the owner thereof, trom
and after the time the personal property is assessed.

Sec. 160. Every tax due upon real property is a lien
against the property assessed; and every tax due upon
improvements upou real estate assessed to others than
the owners of the real estate is a lien upon the land
and improvements, which several liens attach as of the
first day of May in each year,

assessment book and take his receipt for the amount of
tax due thereon. The City Tax Gollector must pub-

1. That the taxes are due, and will become delin-
quent onthe first day of November next thereafter,
und that unless paid prior thereto, five per cent. will be

2. The time and place at which payment of taxes
may be made.

paper printed in the city, for two weeks.

sume to said Treasurer, and must file with the Com-

thereafter. ;

Sec. 165. On the first of November of each year, all
unpaid taxes are delinquent, and no taxes must be col-
lected or received by the Collector froin and after the
first day of November in each year.

Sec. 166. On the sixteenth day of November of each
year, the City Tax Collector must deliver to the Com-
mou Council a true and correct delinquent list of all
persons and property then owing taxes, and at the same
time, also, return the Assessment Roll. In the delin-
quent list so delivered must be set down in numerical
or alpbibetical order all matters and things contained
iu the assessment book and relating to delinquent per-
sonal property. |

Sec. 167. I'he Common Council must carefully com-
pare the delinquent list with the assessment book; and
if satisfied that it contains a full and true statement of
all taxes due and unpaid, they must toot up the total
amount of taxes so remaining unpaid, credit the City
Tax Vollector who acted under it, and make a final

settlement witli him of all taxes charged against him

on the assessment book; and if said settlement does
not correspond with the Treasurer's receipt, they shall
require an immediate account for any existing de-
ficiency.

Sec. 168. After settlement with the Gity Tax Col-
lector, as prescribed in the above section, the Common
Council must add to the delinquent list tive per cent.;
and on the 20th day of November deliver the said list
to the City Tax Collector.

the Common Council, the lien of the city vests in the
purchaser, and is only divested by the payment to him,
or to the Gity Treasurer for his use of the purchase
money and fifty per cent. thereon.

Sec. 177. A redemption of the property sold may be
made by the owner, or any party in interest, within
twelve months from the date of purchase.

Sec. 178. Redemption must be made in gold or sil-
ver coin; and when made to the City Treasurer, he
must credit the amount paid to the person named in

_the Collector’s certificate, and pay it, on demand, to
the person or his assignees, receiving 23 per cent. for
his fees therefor.

an entry in a book similar to that required ¢f the City
Tax Collector.

Sec. 181. On the presentation of the receipt of the
person named in the certificate, or of the City Treas-
urer for his use, of the total amount of redemption
money, the Glerk of the Common Council must mark
the word “Redeemed,” the date, and by who re-
deemed, on the certificate, and in the margin of the
book where the entry of the certificate is made.

Sec. 182. If property is not redeemed within twelve

_ months from the sale, the City Tax Collector must
make, to the purchaser or his assignee, a deed of the
property, reciting in the deed substantially the matters
contained in the certificate, and that no person re-
deemed the property during the time allowed by this
ordinance for its redemption.

Sec. 183. The matters recited in the certificate of
sale must be recited in the deed; and such deed, duly
acknowledged or proved, is primary evidence that—

1. The property was assessed as required by this Or-
dinance.

2. The property was equalized as required by this
Ordinance.

3. The taxes were levied in accordance with this Or-
dinance.

4. The taxes were not paid.

5. Ata proper time and place the property was sold,
as prescribed by this Ordinance, and by the proper
officer.

6. The property was not redeemed.

7. The person who executed the deed was the proper
officer.

8. When the real estate was sold to pay the taxes on
personal property, that the real estate belonged to the
person liable to pay the tax.

Sec. 184. Such deed, duly acknowledged or yroved,
is, except as against actual fraud, -conclusive evidence
of the regularity of all other proceedings from the as-
sessment by the Assessor, inclusive, up to the execu-
tion of the deed.

Sec. 185. The assessment book or delinquent list,
or a copy thereof, certified by the Clerk of the Com-
mon Qouncil, showing unpaid taxes against any person
or property, is primary evidence of the assessment, the

=== PDF PAGE 88 ===
[ 87 |
property assessed, the delinquency, the amount of
taxes due and unpaid, and that all the forms required
by this ordinance in relation to the assessment and levy
of such taxes have been complied with.

See. 186. The City Tax Collector may, after the de-
linquent list is returned to him by the Gommon Coun-
cil, collect the taxes due on personal property, except
when real estate is liable therefor, by seizure and sale
of any personal property owned by the delinquent.
The sale must be at public auction, and of a sufficient
amount of property to pay the taxes, per centage and
costs. The sale must be made after one week’s notice
of the time and place thereof, by publication in a news-

-paper in the city. For signing or selling personal
property, the City Tax Gollector may charge in each
case the sum of two dollars as his fees therefor.

Sec. 187. On payment of the price bid for any prop-
erty sold, the delivery thereof, with a bill of sale, vests
title thereunto in the purchaser. All excess over the
taxes, per cent. and costs of the proceeds of any such
sale must be returned to the owner of the property
sold, and until claimed, must be deposited in the City
Treasury, subject to the owner, heirs or legal represen-
tatives, if demanded within one year; and if not called
for before that time by the person entitled thereto, the
same shall be transferred to the Current Expense
Fund; provided, the same shall be refunded to the
parties entitled thereto, upon demand, without interest.

Sec. 188. The City Tax Collector must annually, on
the first Monday of February, or at the first regular
meeting of the Common Council thereafter, attend

said meeting with the delinquent list; and the Mayor
and Gommon Council must then carefully compare
said delinquent list with the assessments of persons and
property not marked “Paid” on the assessment book;
and when deliquent taxes have been paid, must note
the fact in the appropriate column in the assessment
book. The Tax Oollector shall make an affidavit, ,
which shall be written and subscribed in the delin-
quent list, that every person and all property assessed
in the delinquent list, on which taxes have been paid,
has been credited in the list with such payment. The |
Olerk of the Common Council must then foot up the
amount of taxes remaining unpaid, and credit the City
Tax Collector with the amount and have a final settle-
ment with him; and the delinquent list must remain on

=== PDF PAGE 89 ===
‘
"
; [ 88 ]
file in the Mayor's office. A statement of the unpaid
delinquent taxes must be made out by the Clerk of the
Common Council and filed with the Common Council;
and the Common Council may cancel all taxes which,
in its opinion, cannot be collected, and return said
statement to the City Assessor; and all taxes not can-  —
celled so remaining on said delinquent list must be en-
tered by the Assessor on the assessment book of each
succeeding year until paid or canceled in the way
above provided.

Sec. 189. When land is sold for taxes correctly 1m-
posed as the property of a particular person, no misno-
mer of the owner or supposed owner, or other mistake
relating to the ownership thereof, affects the sale or-  —
renders it void or voidable.

Sec. 190. The City Assessor must collect the taxes
on moveable personal property, when such taxes are
not a lien upon real property, at the time of making
the assessment, or at any time between the first of May
and the first day in August; and he shall make such
collection as is provided for in Section 55 of this Ordi-
nance; and his fees therefor shall be the same as al-
lowed in said Section to the Gity Tax Collector. And
the Assessor shall be governed as to the amount of
taxes to be by him collected on moveable personal
property by the rate of such property assessed on the
previous year. He shall mark “Paid” opposite the
name of persons from whom such tax 1s collected on
the assessment book; and shall, as soon as the assess-
ment is finished, pay into the City Treasury all
moneys. collected by him for such taxes, less the com-
pensation allowed him for making such collection,
taking the Treasurer’s receipt for the same, which he
shall file with the Common Council when he returns
the assessment book.

Sec. 191. All notices required by this ordinance to
be made by publication in a newspaper, may be made
in writing, by posting in three public places in said
city, if so ordered by the Common Gouncil, when the
value in said advertisement does not exceed $10.

Sec. 192. A City Tax Oollector refusing or neglect-
ing to make the payments and settlements required in
this ordinance is liable for the full amount of taxes
charged upon the assessment book. The Gity Attor-
ney must bring suit against the City Tax Gollector and
his sureties for such amount; and in case of neglect,

=== PDF PAGE 90 ===
[ 89 ]
the Gommon Council may require him to do so; and
when the suit is commenced, no credit or allowance
must be made to the Gollector for the taxes outstand-
ing. :

Sec. 193. The Assessor and his sureties are liable on
his official bond for all taxes on property within the
eity limits which, through his willful neglect, is unas-
sessed. The Gity Attorney must, after the Assessor
completes the assessment book for the year, commence
an action on the Assessor's bond for the amount of
taxes lost from such willful failure or neglect. On the
trial of such action, the value of the property unassessed
being shown, judgment for the amount of taxes that
should have been collected thereon must be entered.

Sec. 194. Whenever the time mentioned in this or-
dinance shall be the Sabbath day, then the day follow-
ing shall be considered as the time.

The City Tax Collector shall be allowed two per
cent. on each one hundred dollars of taxes collected by
him.

See. 195. All ordinances and parts of ordinances
heretofore existing on the subject of city revenue or col-
lection of taxes, and especially the ordinance entitled
“Concerning Direct Taxation and the Distribution of
Moneys,” Article Third, P. 6 of the Revised Ordi-
nances of June 1, 1869, and all amendments and supple-
ments thereto, are hereby abrogated and repealed.

See. 196. This Ordinauce shall take effect and be in
force from and after its passage, approval and publica-
tion. Approved June 14, 1872.

CRISTOBAL AGUILAR, Mayor.

Sec. 215. That in all cases of the entry of a judgment
seni net any person (except Indians) of a fine in the
_ Mayor's Gourt, for any violation of the ordinances of
the City of Los Angeles, the Mayor snall have power
to make it a part of the judgment that the defendant
be imprisoned until the fine and costs be paid or satis-
fied; and if said fine and costs are not paid in twelve
hours after such of imprisonment, the Court may make
. it a part of the judgment and sentence: that such per-
sons shall perform labor in the chain gang of said city,
for the residue of said term of imprisonment, and on
the streets or other public works of said city until such
fine and costs shall be discharged at the rate of two
dollars per day; provided that such person may at any
time discharge himself from such imprisonment by
paying the fine and eosts, deducting therefrom the
time he may have performed labor.

Sec. 216. That in all cases of the entry of a judg-
ment against any Indian for drunkenness, or other vio-
lation of any of the ordinances of said city whenever a
judgment or fine is entered, the Mayor shall have
poet to make it a part of the judgment: that the de-

endant be imprisoned until the fine and costs be paid

or satisfied, or shall, in the discretion of the Mayor,
sentence such Indian to labor in the chain gang of said
city, and on the streets and other public works, or
other labor in the city limits of said city until such fine
and costs shall be paid, at the rate of fifty cents per
day; provided, that the fine shall not be less than two
dollars and fifty cents, and not exceeding five dollars;
and provided further, that such Indian may at any
time discharge himself by paying the residue of the
fine, with costs.

Sec. 217. For the arrest of any person, except an In-
dian, there shall be charged and allowed the sum of
two dollars, in coin, to the person making the arrest,
and such other fees to the Mayor of the city as is al-
lowed by law for like services in Justices’ Courts of
this State, in all cases where the same is collected from
the defendant; but in no case shall the city be liable,
or in any manner bound to pay the costs in such action
or proceedings; provided, the sum of fifty cents per
day to the Jailor, for the keeping and boarding of such
persons, shall be allowed for the time such persons
shall be in prison or perform labor; and, also, the fur-
ther sum of fifty cents shall be paid to the Jailor as a

=== PDF PAGE 92 ===
i
[91]
compensation for committing prisoners to jail in the
night, provided the same is collected from the person
under arrest.

Sec. 218. All fines shall be payable in coin; and all
fines collected by the Mayor, or other officer of the
city, shall be paid into the City Treasury, and the same
shall be reported at each meeting of the City Council
to said Council.

Sec. 219. In all cases hereafter of a violation of the
ordinances of said city, where a fine is entered as the
judgment for the second offence against the same per-
son, it shall be the duty of the officer rendering such
judgment to increase the fine to double the amount of
the first offence.

Sec. 220. That Sections 1, 2 and 8, Article XII. of
the revised ordinances of Los Angeles, passed and ap-
proved June 1, 1869, and so much of Section 5, of Ar-
ticle V. of the said revised ordinances, as provides a
punishment by work in such manner and place as the
Mayor shall direct, not exceeding ten days for the sec-

ond offence, be, and the same is hereby repealed; and
all other ordinances in conflict with this ordinance are
hereby repealed. Approved April 15, 1872.

AN ORDINANCE
Granting certain privileges in the City of Los Angeles to

the Southern Pacific Railroad Company.

The Mayor and Gonimon Council of the City of Los

Angeles do ordain as follows:

Section 1. That the right of way for the railroad -
track of the Southern Pacific Railroad Company in and
out of the City of Los Angeles, for the distance said
Company may wish to use the same, be, and the same
is hereby granted to said Gompany, its successors and
assigns, over and upon the street and its extension,
commonly called Alameda street. The said Company

to locate its track or tracks as near the center of the
street as may be; and the residue of said street to be
held and used by said city for the passage of persons
and vehicles on each side of the same; provided, that
said city expressly reserves the right to locate cross
streets over and across any part of Alameda street and
railroad track, as the future improvements and con-
venience of the city may require; and the Railroad
Company, in consideration of said right of way, agree
to provide and keep in repair convenient crossings

=== PDF PAGE 94 ===
3
[ 93 J

over their track or tracks at the crossing of such streets
now in use, or which may be hereafter located by said
city. And provided further, that said city reserves the
right to construct and pass under said railroad track
any water canal or zanja, or water ditch, at the expense
of said city, its successors or assigns, which may be-
come necessary for the use of the inhabitants of said
city. The said Railroad Company, its saccessors and
assigns, to correspond the track of said road to the re-
quired grade of water canals, zanjas or ditches now in
use; and, provided further, that said railroad track or
tracks shall correspond, to the official grade of said
street.

Sec. 2. Said Company are authorized to build over
or under any of the city zanjas; provided, that the !
same are put ina safe and secure operating condition
at such crossings, by and at the expense of the com- -
pany, wherever such crossing interfere with the exist-
ing channels.

Sec. 3. For purposes of information to be acquired,
the said company are hereby empowered to enter upon
any street, lane or alley, or other city property, for pur-
poses of survey and with engineering parties, and to
examine and survey any waters within the control of
the city. Approved Sept. 6th, 1872.

CRISTOBAL AGUILAR, Mayor.

with one thousand feet of hose; but the Common
Council shal] have power to increase the number of
Fire Companies as the public safety of the city may
require, and to purchase the necessary steam engines,
apparatus and appurtenances therefor.

Sec. 236. The only engines that shall be purchased
by the city and used by the Fire Department, shall be
steam fire engines of the best pattern and manufacture.

Sec. 237. A Steam Fire Engine Company shali be

twenty-five males, of full age—of twenty-one years; all

partment. A company desiring admission into the

Council of the City of Los Angeles, accompanying
such application with a copy of their constitution,
signed by at least twenty-five males, of the age of
twenty-one years, the names of their officers, and
where they desire to be located. Ifsuch application re-
ceives the approval of the Common Council, the com-
pany shall be declared admitted into the Department,
and entitled to the privileges of the same.

Sec. 242. It shall be the duty of each owner or
driver of every hack, coach or carriage to procure of
the Glerk of the Gity Council, the respective number
for each hack, coach or carriage, and place the same in. ~
some conspicuous place on such hack, coach, or car-
riage. It shall also be the duty of such owner or
driver to have lamps lighted on their vehicles, always at
night, while engaged in conveying passengers through
the streets of the city; also, to have printed rates of fare
posted in some conspicuous })lace inside of each vehicle.

See. 243. No hackman, coach, carriage or omnibus
driver shall be permitted to drive at an immoderate
gait or dangerous speed within the streets of said city,

- norin any case permit their horses to gallop or run,
nor in any case to race in said streets of the city; nor
shall any person be permitted to drive any such car-
riages, hacks, omnibuses or coaches, under the age of
eighteen years; and each hackman shall wear a badge,
designating the number of his hack, in a conspicuous
place; and each and every hack driver, before he shall
be permitted to drive any hack, coach, carriage or om-
nibus, shall pay a license of five dollars, which shall be
good for one year from the date thereof to the person
named in such license, which sum of five dollars shall
be paid to the City Marshal, for the use of the city, as
other licenses; and the said Marshal shall, on payment,
procure and deliver a license to such person therefor.

Sec. 244. It is hereby declared unlawful for any per-

- son or persons to leave unfastened and unattended to,
auy horse, either attached to any vehicle or not, on the -
public streets of Los Angeles; but it shall be the duty
of all persons to safely secure their horses, and if found
unhitched, it shall be the duty of the police officers of
the city to secure said animals and to arrest the own-
ers thereof; and any person violation the provisions of
this. Section shall be liable to a fine of not less than
five dollars, nor more than twenty-five dollars, on con-
viction thereof before any Court of competent jurisdic-

CHAPTER XVIII.

TO SECURE THE SAFE KEEPING OF
REGORDS, MAPS AND ARCHIVES OF THE
CITY.

SECTION——. | SEcTION——.

247. City Archives, how Sec. 248. Glerk to make
kept—not to be removed copies. Persons may copy
except in certain cases. and examine.

Sec. 247. That it shall not be, hereafter, lawful for
any one to carry away from the office of the Clerk of
the City, or from the Council room of said city, any
record, map, or any part of the archives of said city,
unless when commanded by a subpana duces tecum; nor
shall it be lawful for any one having the custody of said
maps, records or archives, to permit the same to be
carried away trom said office, or Common Gouncil
building, by any other person than the person having
the legal custody thereof, except for the preservation
of the same, in case of threatened loss by fire; and any
person violating this Section, shall, on conviction, be
fined not less than five nor more than twenty-five dol-
lars for each offense.

Sec. 248. It shall be the duty of the Clerk to make
and certify any copies of all such records, upon pay-
ment of a just and reasonable compensation therefor;
provided, that said records, maps, or archives may be
examined and copied by any person desiring to do so,
free of charge. Approved June 28, 1872.

SECTION——. SECTION——.

Sec. 249. There shall be, and there hereby is estab-
lished, a Board of Health for the City of Los Angele‘s
said Board of Health, consisting of five persons, to be
appointed by the Mayor, with the advice and consent
of the Gommon Council.
| Sec. 250. The said city shall be divided into five dis-
tricts, as follows:
First District—From Moran’s Lane and Frohling’s,
consisting of all the city lands to the south and west of
these lanes, and all lands to the south and west of the
line formed by producing Prohling’s Lane across—and :
perpendicular to Alameda street. |
Second District—All city grounds west of Main —
street, and to the northwest corner of the Plaza tothe —
centre of Church street.
Third District—A1l city grounds east of Main street,
and to Los Angeles river, uj) to Aliso street, and the
southeast corner of the Plaza, and north of Moran’s 4
and Frohling’s Lanes, atoresaid.
Fourth District—All the city lands north of the 7
Plaza, Church street, and Aliso street, and on the west |
side of Los Angeles river.
Fifth District—All the city lands east of Los Angeles
river.
Sec. 251. The Mayor, in appointing the Board of
Health, shall designate in weich district each appointee
is to act; and it shall not be necessary for such mem-
ber to be a resident of the district for which he is to
act, but all the members must reside within the city.

=== PDF PAGE 104 ===
[103 ]

Sec. 252. It shall be the duty of said Board of
Health—each one for the district for which he is to act,
to see that any and all persons, without respect to age
or color, who are sick with the small-pox, and not con-
valescent, or who may hereafter be sick with the same,
and have not the means of paying therefor, be provided
by the city with medicine, food and medical attention;
provided, that in all such cases, such persons shall be
immediately removed to the City Hospital; provided,
such Hospital has sufficient capacity for such persons;
and none of the members of said Board of Health shall
be authorized to furnish any food, clothing, medicine
or medical attention to any person so long as accommo-
dations are furnished in any city hospital for said per-
sons.

Sec. 253. It shall further be the duty of each com-
missioner of said Board, within their respective dis-
ticts, to see that every person, without distinction of
age, sex or color, who has not had the kine-pox, or
smail-pox, be properly vaccinated. The fact of their
having had such kine-pox, or small-pox, shall be |
evinced by marks upon their persons, and when any
person not having had the kine, or small-pox, shall re-
fuse, upon request of such Commissioner, to attend up-
on the city surgeon or physician, who shall be ex-offi-
cio, a member of the Board of [fealth, for the purpose
of beine vaccinated, then it shall be the duty of said
Commissioner to arrest all sueh persons and carry them
by force, if necessary, before such city surgeon or
physician for the purpose of being vaccinated, and for
the purpose of such arrest, each and every Marshal,
city policeman, and all their deputies, can be called to
the aid of said Commissioner, to assist him in making
said arrest; and it is hereby made the duty of all
Marshals, policemen, and their deputies, to obey said
Commissioner when called upon.

Sec. 254. That when any person called upon by such
Commissioner, shall neglect or refuse to go before the
surgeon or physician of the city, for the purpose of
such vaccination, it shall be the duty of such Commis-
sioner to report the same to the Mayor, who shall im-
mediately issue a warrant for the arrest of such person,
and cause him or her, or them, to be brought before
him, and shall proceed immediately to hear and invest-
igate the case; and if such person be found guilty of
such offence, then such person shall be deemed guilty

=== PDF PAGE 105 ===
a
[104 ]
of a misdemeanor, and shall be fined in a sum not less
than ten nor more than fifty dollars, or imprisoned not
less than five nor more than ten days, or by both such
fine and imprisonment, at the discretion of the Mayor.

See. 255. Said Commissioners shall be paid out of
the city fund such compensation as the Mayor and
Common GQouncil shall deem reasonable. Approved |
June 1, 1869.

Sec. 259. It is made the duty of the Mayor, withthe ;

advice of the Committee on Police, to have a map or
plot made of the east or front half of the public city
eemetery, which shall be laid out into whole lots, half
lots and quarter lots; one whole lot to be sixteen by ten
(16x10) feet in dimensions, one half lot to be eight by
ten (8x10) feet, and one quarter lot to be four by ten
(4x10) feet.
- Sec. 260. There shall be a Sexton, appointed by the
Mayor, with the approval of the Gommon Council,
who shall hold his office during the pleasure of the
Mayor and Common Council, or until his successor is
appointed and qualified; and said Sexton shall take the
oath of office, and give bonds in such sum as the Com-
mon Council shall direct for the faithful performance
of his duties, and s1id Sexton shall be allowed for his
services an annual salary of one hundred ($100) dollars,
to be paid in equal instalments monthly.

See. 261. It shall be the duty of said Sexton—first,
to tuke charge of said cemetery, and to attend all
burials within its enclosure. Second, to take charge
of the keys of the gate, and to keep said cemetery gate
closed every day of the week except Sunday, on which
day he shail open the said gate at sunrise and allow the
same to remain open until sunset.

Sec. 262. He shall dig any graves, and inter any
dead body in said cemetery when applied to for that
purpose, and for such service may charge, not to ex-
ceed the sum of six ($6) dollars; and said Sexton, by
virtue of his office, is hereby declared to be ex-officio a
policeman of said city.

Sec. 262. Any person who shall tear down, injure or
destroy the feuce around said;cemetery, or any enclos-
ure inside ofthe same, or cause the same to be injured
or destroyed, or injure any shade, ornamental or other
tree or trees therein, shall upon conviction, be punished
by fine not less than ten dollars, nor to exceed two

CHAPTER XXI.
GAS WORKS.

SccTlon——. } SEcTION——.

264. Grant to James Works.

Walsh. _ 268. Ratification of pre-

office, free of cost. matter of Gasa Nuisance;

lute. lic sewer.

Sec. 264. There is hereby granted and given to James
Walsh, of San Francisco, his heirs or assigns, the sale and
exclusive right and privilege for the period of twenty
years, of erecting gas works within the limits of the City
of Los Angeles; the place where, and character of said
works, to be at the discretion of said James Walsh, his
heirs or assigns; and there is hereby granted to said
James Walsh, the right of way to lay down pipes in
all the public streets of said city; and the said City of
Los Angeles hereby obligates itself not to make a
grant or franchise to any other person than the said
James Walsh, for the period of twenty years, for the
purposes above stated. Provided, and all the forego-
ing grant and franchises are made with these express
provisions, to wit: That no private property shall be
interfered with, and that the erection of said gas works
shall commence within two years from this date, and
shall be completed at the end of three years therefrom,

=== PDF PAGE 108 ===
=
[107 ]
and at a cost of not less than five thousand dollars,
otherwise the grant hereby made to be void.

Sec. 265. The said James Walsh, his heirs and as-
signs, are hereby required to furnish the Mayor's office
with gas free of charge.

. Sec. 266. Whereas a grant was conditionally made
to James Walsh by an ordinance of the Mayor and
Gommon Council of Los Angeles City, passed and ap-
proved the 5th day of March, A. D. 1866, and by a
contract of same date, referring to said ordinance and
repeating and confirming the said conditional grant;
the effect of the said ordinance and contract being to
grant a certain franchise to said Walsh, his heirs and
assigns, for manufacturing and selling gas in said city
upon certain terms and conditious expressed in said or-
dinance and contract; and particularly upon condition
that said Walsh, his heirs or assigns, should, within a
prescribed time, erect certain contemplated gas works,
at a cost of not less than four thousand dollars; and,
whereas, the contemplated works have been execuied
and completed, and at a cost far exveeding the amount
required, and in good and satisfactory manner; and
whereas, all other conditions of said grant have been
complied with by said Walsh, his heirs and assigns, so
far as contemplated and practicable up to this time;
wherefore, it is ordained, that said conditional grant,
according to its terms and tenor, has become and now
is absolute.

Sec. 267. The gas works above referred to have been
erected in all respects conformably to the spirit and in-
tention of the said ordinance and contract, and the site
thereof ha; been well selected, and is hereby approved.

Sec. 268. The previous ordinance and contract here-
in referred to are hereby ratified and confirmed in all
their provisions. Approved June 1, 1869.

Wuereas, The s:wage matter of the Los Angeles
Gas Works constitute a nuisance in the public sewer of
the city of Los Angeles.

It is hereby Ordained, that the said sewage matter
of said gas works shall no longer be emptied into the
public sewer, and the Los Angeles Gas Gompany is
hereby ordered to cut off their connection with said
public sewer, and if said connection be not so cut off,
the City Marshal or any other officer or person the
Gity Council shall direct, is here ordered and directed
forthwith to sever said connection.

Sec. 292. The work of improving, grading, paving 7
or otherwise improving the streets or parts of streets, ’
sidewalks, and cross walks of the streets of the city of
Los Angeles, shall be done under the direction of a
Board of Public Works. This Board shall consist of
five members of the Gommon Council. Said Board
shall have charge, superintendence and management of )
the grading, filling, paving and all other improvements
of the streets of said city, and of the construction of
the sidewalks and cross walks of the streets of said city; —
provided, that the Gity Council shal! at all times di-
rect upon what streets and avenues such, and what im-
provements shall be made, and shall be and are hereby
authorized to make such contract, to employ and dis-
charge from time to time, such superintendents, work-
men and teams as they may deem necessary, and pay
the persons employed such salaries and wages as they
shall deem proper. Three of said Board shall consti-
tute a quorum to do business, and the acts of a majori-
ty of those present shall be binding upon the whole.
They shall keep a record of their acts and doings; shall
keep and preserve copies of all contracts, estimates,
plans, ete., and shall report their acts and doings in
detail to the City Council at the first regular meeting
of each month. ‘
See. 293. At the first regular meeting after each year-
ly election for Councilmen, there shall be appointed
members of the Board of Public Works to succeed
those whose terms of oflice will then expire: and such
appointments shall be for one year each. Should a
vacancy oceur, it shall befilled at any regular meeting
of the Council, and a member appointed shall hold his
office tor the unexpired term. The Mayor shall be

=== PDF PAGE 130 ===
a
[129 }
ex-officio a member, and the City Clerk shall be Sec-
retary of the Board of Public Works.

Sec. 294. Within ten days after the appointment of
the new members of said Board, and notice of their
appointment shall have been given to them by the Gi-
ty Clerk, the members of said Board of Public Works
shal] proceed to organize by appointing a President.

Sec. 295. The Board of Public Works shall take an
oath of office, that they are not now, nor will be inter-
ested, directly or indirectly, in any contract entered in-
to by the city for the improvement on any street, ave-
nue, alley, sidewalk or cross-walk; provided, that this
shall not apply to any person who is desirous to im-
prove the street or sidewalk in front of his property.

See. 296. Ifthe appointments, as members of the
Board of Public Works, are not accepted within five
days after the publication of the proceedings of the
City Council by which the appointments were made,
such as fail to accept, shall be considered as not accept-
ing, and the Council shall fill the vacancies at their

next meeting. by new appointments.

Sec. 297. Whenever the city of Los Angeles shall
by resolutions or ordinance, direct and authorize the
filling, grading, paving or planking, or refilling, re-
grading, repaving, replanking, or repairing of any
street or avenue, or any portion of any street or ave-
nue including the construction of sidewalks, cross-
walks and footwalks, the Board of Public Works may.
if they deem it necessary, cause a survey, plat or pro-
file, and specification and estimates of the contemplat-
ed work, to be made and deposited with their Secreta-
ry. When so deposited, or when any work is ordered
by the Board, the Secretary of the Board of Public
Works suall give notice for ten days in the newspaper
publishing the ordinances of the city, or by posting up
4 notices in 4 public places of the city,two of which shall
be contiguous to the contemplated work, that such pro-
files, specifications or orders for work are deposited in
his office, and are open for inspection and examination,
and shal] at the same time, and in the same manner,
and for the same length of time advertise for proposals
for doing said work, stating the time within which said
work shall be completed.

The bids for the doing of such work shall be sealed
bids, directed to the Board of Public Works of the
citv of Los Angeles, and shall be open at the hour and

place mentioned in said notice, by said Board of Pub- —
lic Works. When the bids for doing the proposed
work shall have been opened, the contract for doing
the same shall be awarded to the lowest responsible
bidder or bidders, who will sufficiently guarantee to |
the satisfaction of said Board, within such time as may
be fixed by contract for the faithful completion of the i
work, as provided in the contract.

title the holder or holders thereof to receive the amount
that may be due thereon from the assessment to be
collected from the property thereon made.

An Ordinance permanently establishing the lines and
names of certain streets in the city of Los Angeles.
The Mayor, Recorder and Common Council of the City

of Los Angeles do ordain as follows :

The western line of Main street in said city shall re-
main as shown by the plat of a survey made on the
24th day of May A. D. 1854, by Henry Hancock,
County Surveyor of Los Angeles county, State of Gal-
ifornia, which said plat is now filed in the office of the
Mayor of said city; said Main street being ninety-three
(95 feet in width through its whole length from the
corner of Jose Salazar’s house to the house marked
on said plat as of Jesus Dominguez and the alley
marked in said plat between said Jast mentioned house
aud the lot of the Honorable Benjamin Hayes, shall re-
main as shown on said plat, being thirteen and eight-
tenth varas in width, having its co..rse along said lot of
the said Hayes, as shown on said plat, extending from
Main street back to the summit of the hill commonly
ealled Fort Hill; and is hereby declared to be a public
street, and named Bridge street.

A publie street is hereby established back of Main
street west, as shown in said plat of survey, being sev-
e ity feet in width and extending from W. T. B. San-
ford’s gate ina course north 803 degrees east to the
southwest corner of the churchyard enclosed by an
adobe wall, and which is hereby named New High
street.

The lines of Los Angeles street are hereby estab-
lished as follows, viz: Its western line shall run ona
direct line from the corner of William Nordhold’s
house, wi.h the porch of the house on the west side of
the house of Thomas Sanchez to where it intersects
the cross street between the property of Alexander
Belland Abel Stearns; and its eastern line shall be es-
tablished from the house of Felipe Khein to an angle
in the house of Manuel Reqeuna, fronting Franklin

Sec. 349. That hereafter all persons who shall be

other officer or person, as the City Council may ap. -

detail one of the policemen of the city, and it is hereby
made the duty of such policemen to execute and dis-
charge all the orders of the Superintendent of Public
Wath as respects the employment of convicts, and
such policeman shall take charge of said chain-gang,
or persons so sentenced to labor.

fence, on conviction thereof be fined in not less than
the fine of such offenders so permitted to escape, with
costs of the action, before any Court of competent
jurisdiction.

Sec. 354. Persons sentenced to perform such labor
may discharge themselves at any time on payment of ~
the amount which may be due of the fine and costs, if
any such shall be due.

Sec. 355. The Jailor shall receive from the city for
boarding and lodging such prisoners the sum of fifty

| cents per day each, and it shall be his duty to report to
the Gity Marshal or person having the chain-gang in
charge, all persons in his custody sentenced to perform
labor in said city.

Sec. 356. The prisoners shall be treated with the ut-

all rules and regulations prescribed to comjiel dicip-

No. 1.
An Ordinance concerning the Municipal lands.
The Common Gouncil of the City of Los Angeles, do
; ordain as follows: :

Sec. 1. All the vacant lands belonging to the city of
Los Angeles, except those hereafter enumerated, may
be disposed of in conformity with this ordinance.

Sec. 2. Any person who may desire to procure lands
belonging tothe city for the purpose of cultivation,
shall present his petition to the Mayor of the city, set-

ting forth accurately the description by metes and
bounds, as near as may be, and the length and _ bredth
by yards, ofthe land applied for, together with the
names of the owners or possessors of land in the imme-
diate neighborhood.

Sec. 3. Upon the receipt of the petition, the Mayor
shall cause the Marshal to notify the owners or posses-
sors of lands in the neighborhood, of the presentation
of the petition, who shall be entitled to appear and con-
test the application, upon a day to be fixed by the
Mayor, which shall not be less than one week after fil-
ing the petition.

Sec. 4. Upon the day fixed, the Mayor, if he find
that there are no conflicting claims, and that it is oth-
erwise fit and proper, may grant a certificate of posses-
sion to the petitioner, which shall have reference to this
ordinance, whereby he shall be entitled to enter and
possess the said tract of land for the term of one year
from the date of his certificate.

Sec. 5. In no case shall a lot of more than ten acres
within the limits of the city, or thirty-five acres with-
out said limits, be granted as aforesaid by the Mayor.

Sec. 6. Upon the presentation of any petition as afore-
said, the petitioner shal] pay to the Mayor ten dollars
to pay the expenses incurred by his application; of
said sum the Marshal shall receive two dollars when-
ever there isa notification to the neighboring owners

| or possessors; the Mayor shall receive four dollars in
full for his services, and the balance shall be deposited

=== PDF PAGE 153 ===
[152 ] | &
in the.city treasury to the eredit of the land fund, and ”
shall be used as the Common Council shall thereafter |
determine. ‘ |

Sec. 7. No person shall be entitled to obtain more 4a
than two certificates of possession under the provisions Cru
of this ordinance, nor shall the number of acres granted -
to any person by the said one or two certificates ever i
exceed the quantity prescribed in section 5, to-wit: In ii
all, thirty-five acres. be

Sec. 8. The Mayor shall keep, in a well bound book, Tie
a record of all his proceedings under this ordinance, =~
and shall file all the petitions for lands in his office,and =
at every meeting of the Common Council on or subse-
quent to the first day of each month, shall transmit a re- “a
port of all the certificates of possession which he may 2
have issued, which shall be considered approved unless *
the Common Council, by resolution written one month o
after his said report, annul or disallow them, in which , 3
case they shall be cunsidered of no eflect. a

Sec. 9. Immediately after the qualification of the
new members of the Common Gouncil elected at a
general municipal election, they shall name -by ballot
three of their numbers as a Board of Adjudication of
municipal lands, who shall have power, upon the peti-
tion of the grantees of the certificates of possession, to
hear, at the expiration of one year from the date of
their granting by the Mayor, their application to have
a title in fee simple, made out to them by the city.

Sec. 10. If upon the hearing of said petition, the
Board be satisfied that the said petitioner in good faith ‘
has improved the tract or tracts of municipal land de— F
seribed in his certificate of possession, by building, - =
planting orange or other fruit trees, or cultivating crops ’
thereon, provided, however, that said improvements
shall never be less than two hundred dollars, they shall ‘
certify said facts to the Mayor, who is hereby author- |
ized to execute and acknowledge a deed of the land
described in his certificate or certificates of possession |
to the petitioner in the name or on the behalf of the
city; which said deed shall specially refer to this. ordin- |
ance and be subject to the conditions herein contained.

Sec. 11. But if the said Board shall be satisfied that !
the said improvements have not been made in good
faith, they shall also certify this fact to the Mayor, who
shall be authorized to issue a certificate of possession
to any party applying therefor, for the land described

=== PDF PAGE 154 ===
¥
[153 ]}

in the certificate of the petitioner to said Board.

| Sec. 12. If any person, within one year from the
date of this certificate; shall fail to make the proofs as
set forth in section ten, the Mayor may grant posses-
sion to said land to any person applying, as of vacant
city land: Provided, nevertheless, that if the original
holder-of the first certificate be in possession, he shall,
on petition, be entitled to a preference.

Sec. 13. The conditions upon which all of said grants
and deeds shall be made, are, that upon said lands the
city shall always have the right to establish public
roads, and. cross-roads, sufficient for four-wheel car-
riages, and in whatever direction may be necessary,
with acqueducts and canals of water for common use,
common sewers and private irrigation, and all owners
shall facilitate the entrance into said lands, to the offi-
cers encharged with the conservation, distribution, and
arrangements of the water, and to the laborers engaged
in the cleansing and conservation of the acqueducts,
nor prohibiting the neighbors from entering to direct
and facilitate the passage of the waters to their fields
or gardens when it corresponds to them by ordinances,
and the favored persons, at all times, although they
may be out of the limits of the jurisdiction of the city,
shall pay whatever tax that is or may be established
for the use of the water, the same as that paid by resi-
dents and owners within said limits, and shall be sub-
ject and will conform to whatever general system of
water that may be established by competent author-
ity. A violation of these conditions will cause the
owner a forfeiture of his right to the land granted, or |
confirmed by title, and the land shall be subject to
confiseation, for the benefit of the City of Los Angeles.

Sec. 14. After having practiced all due dilligence,
as prescribed in this ordinance, to see if the land is
public unoecupied land, if there is no obstacle to the
adjudication, it shall be made, measuring the land, ob-
serving between himself and others the corresponding
boundaries, demarking there upon the ground the
spaces that are to remain for streets or passage-ways, :
assisting in the operation one or more individuals of
the commission.

Sec. 15. Allimproved lands, all lands blocked or
lined off on the city plan, the hill called Fort Hill, the
hill in rear'of the house of Pio Pico, are hereby ex-
cepted from the provisions of this Ordinance.

=== PDF PAGE 155 ===
3
(154 |

Sec. 16. The lands comprehended in the certificates
and which are the object of this ordinance, cannot be
sold, pledged, nor in any manner alienated but by the
permission of the Common Council, except when the
owner shall have perfected his title. |

See. 17. Those whose lands shall approach within

| fifty yards of the edge of the river, shall have the lib-
erty of occupying up to river’s bank, which adjudi-
cation shall be made in conformity with this ordinance,
and no certificate shall be given for land facing on
the river for more than four hundred yards.

Sec. 18. All lands embraced in the certificates ot
possession, issued under this ordinance, shall be in
form as nearly square as possible. |

See. 19. That the Mayor be empowered to grant, on
application, all building lots belonging to the city, sub-
ject to the provisions heretofore set forth in this ordi-
nance—excepting squares already reserved for public
use.

Sec. 20. This ordinance shall take effect from the day
of its publication. |

No, 2.

An ordinance to repeal an ordinance concerning
municipal lands, approved August 13, A. D., 1852.

The Mayor, Recorder and Common Council of the
City of Los Angeles do ordain as follows:

Sec. 1. The ordinance entitled an ordinance concern-
ing municipal lands, approved August 13, A. D. 1852,
is hereby repealed.

Sec. 2. The lands and lots, the complete titles to
which are yet pending, shall, at the proper time, be
adjudged to the persons. to whom they have been
granted, if they comply with the provisions of said or-
dinance, otherwise the said lands and lots shall revert
to the city.

An ordinance to determine the front line of the houses
of this city which are separated from the streets in the
delineation and formation of the plan of this city.

The Mayor, Recorder and Common Gounceil of the
city of Los Angeles do ordain as follows:

Sec. 1. The houses whose fronts are separated from
the streets of the city since the plan of the city has
been formed, can establish their fronts on the line of
the nearest street, at the choice of the owner, and the
city relinquishes in favor of the owners of such houses
all the right it may have to the land between the
house and the street.

Sec. 2. This ordinance shall take effect from the
date of its publication, and repeals the municipn! de-
cree of March 23, 1854.
