No. 4.
An Ordinance Establishing the Forms and Requisites
in the Alienation of City Lots and Lands.

The Mayor, Recorder and Gommon Council of the
City of Los Angeles do ordain as follows:

Sec. 1, All persons that wish to purchase from the
City of Los Angeles lots or lands shall deposit in the
office of the Mayor a description notice of the lot or
lands they desire to purchase, setting forth the loca-
tion, boundary and dimensions exactly, as may be
shown upon the city map and survey of adjacent lands.

Sec. 2. Upon the first Wednesday of each month, af-
ter notice of sale has been given for two weeks prior
thereto, by posting up the same, in writing, in at least
three conspicuous places in the city, one of which shall
be on the door of the Mayor’s office, the Mayor shall
offer at public auction, at his office, for cash in hand,

=== PDF PAGE 157 ===
[156 | 3

between: the hours of ten and twelve of the forenoon, 3
the lots and lands that shall have beem solicited during
the previous month, selling the, same /to the, highest
bidder. : The hours of sale designated in this section, at
the discretion of the Mayor, may be extended until 4
o'clock P. M.

Sec. 8. The Mayor shall receive the purchase
money, and give the purchaser a quit-claim, and shall
note the same in the book of record, wherein are pre- |
served concessions of municipal lands, since the 13th
of August, A. D., 1852.
~ Sec. 4. The Mayor shall receive four dollars for  ,
each deed he shall extend in obedience to this ordi- bs
nance, which shall be deducted from the amount of y
sales, and shall pay the remainderinto the city treasury. |

See. 5. The lots of land to which this ordinance has 2
reference are those of one hundred and twenty feet
front, by one hundred and sixty-five deep, numerically
numbered in parallelograms, and also numbered upon
the map of the city; the lands are those of thirty-five
acres, designated in the same manner upon the city sur-
vey.

Sec. 6. All lands with improvements are exempt
from the operation of this ordinance, and the Jots 3 and
8 of each parallelogram upon the city map which shal]
not have been previously disposed of before the ap-
proval of this ordinance; Fort Hill, and the Round
Hill, in the rear of the house of Mr. Pio Pico, and the
lands that have useful stone’ for building, or lime, are
all exceptions, and not liable to sale under the provis- ~~
ions of this ordinance: Provided, that nothing in this
ordinance shall prevent the Common Council from
disposing of her public domain from time to time, as
may be thought necessary, to meet the demands of the
public and private utility.

Sec. 7. This ordinance shall take effect from its ap- |
proval.

Los Angeles, July 7, 1854.

after the day of its publication.

RULES OF THE POLICE DEPARTMENT.

lst. The Marshal is chief of police, and shall have
the entire management and control of the police, both
regular and special.

2d. If any officer shall refuse to obey any order, or
follow the instructions given by the chief, or commit
any misdemeanor, the chief shall suspend him from
duty until the next regular meeting of the Council,
when the charge shall be preferred against him; and
if found guilty, shall be discharged from the force.
3d. All members of the department shall devote
their whole time and attention to the business of the
department, and not follow any other calling; and al-
though certain hours are allotted to each man’s duty,
on ordinary occasions, yet all the members must be
prepared ‘to act-at a moment's notice, whenever their
servicesanay berequired: =
_4th.: P wnctual attendance shall be — of every’
officer on all occasions; sickness and disability only

=== PDF PAGE 167 ===

=== PDF PAGE 168 ===
[167 |

see that the necessary affidavits are made, and wit-
nesses subpcenaed at the opening of the Court. '

15th. Officers will not be allowed to leave their
beats for the purpose of attending balls, places of
amusement, etc., without the permission of the chief.
No special policeman shall discharge any police duty
unless his star is prominently in view, under penalty of
having his warrant revoked.

16th. On the occurrance of fire in the city, all the

é officers on duty are to repair immediately to the scene
os of conflagration, and report to the chief of police, and
ae ai(i in preserving order, preventing the crowd from ob-
structing the citizens engaged in extinguishing the
flames, and in guarding property, and arresting all per-
; sons caught stealing or committing other depredations.
17th. Police officers shall take cognizance of the
small boys who roam the street at night, and if any are
found without parents or guardians leading idle and
dissolute lives, to bring them before the Mayor for ex-
amination.

18th. Members of the department must be civil and
respectful to the public, and upon all occasions per-
form their duties with good temper, discretion, and
firmness.

Forbearance under provocation, and a temperate
though firm deportment is what is needed. Every po-
liceman must recollect that, in making an arrest, he is
not justified in using more force than is actually neces-
sary for the safe custody of the parties until he conveys
them to their proper destination.

It is the duty of a policeman to keep prisoners safely;
but he has no right to punish them, and he must not
even use language calculated to offend or provoke
them. A policeman must be cautious never to inter-

| fere unnecessarily; but when requested to act, he
should do so with discretion, decision, and boldness;
and he may arrest any one who opposes him in the ex-
ecution of his duty. Arranged by
Wu. C. WARREN, .
| Chief of Police. —

with gas. The money derived from said tax shall be
set apart for and be known as the Gas Fund, and shall
be used for no other purpose whatsoever. '

APPENDIX No. B.
eaQSCrer
No. 1.
GAS SEWERAGE.

The Mayor and Gommon Gouncil of the City of Los
Angeles do ordain as follows:

Sec. 1. Whereas, the sewage matter of the Los An-
geles Gas works constitute a nuisance in the public
sewer of the city of Los Angeles, it is hereby ordained
that the said sewage matter of said Gas Works shall no -
longer be emptied into the public sewer, and the Los
Angeles Gas Gompany is hereby ordered to cut off
their connection with said public sewer, and if said
connection be not so cut off, the Gity Marshal or any
other officer or person the City Council shall direct, is
here ordered and directed forthwith to sever said
connection.

Sec. 2. This ordinance to be in force from and after
its passage and approval and publication, and all ordi-
nances in conflict with this ordinance be, and the same
is hereby repealed.

Approved 19th August, 1872.

CRISTOBAL AGUILAR.
Mayer.

question of the sale and assignment of the stock held

ence to annex the same thereto,” approved February

prove of the result of said election. :

thence N. 593°, E. one hundred feet; thence N. 652°,
E. one hundred feet; thence N. 713°, E. one hundred
feet; thence N. 774°, E. one hundred feet; thence N.
834°, E. five thousand four hundred feet, across the

=== PDF PAGE 198 ===
[197 ]
river of Los Angeles to east line of Elijah Moulton’s
orchard; thence 82°, E. one hundred feet; thence N.
813°, E. one hundred feet; thence N. 80°, E. one hun-
dred feet; thence 78}°, E. one hundred feet; thence
N. 783°, E. two thousand one hundred and sixty-
five feet to the southeast line of Elijah Moulton’s tract
| of land, is hereby declared a public street of the city

of Los Angeles, and open as such to the use of the
public, and same be designated and known as Mission
street.

See. 2. The City Attorney is instructed to institute
proceedings in the proper Court for the condemna-

| tion of all of the above described land, or so much

thereofas it may be necessary to condemn for the
opening of said street. |

Sec. 3. All ordinancesand parts of ordinances, so far
as they conflict with this ordinance, shall be, and the
same are hereby repealed.

This ordinance shall take effect and be in force
from and after its passage, approval and publication.

Approved March 4, 1873.

J.R. TOBERMAN, Mayor.
